People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I’ve definitely felt down a lot over the past couple weeks, but I haven’t really kept track of whether it’s most of the day each day.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt hopeless?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve felt pretty low at times, but “hopeless” feels harder to pin down. I don’t know if I’d say that’s been there clearly over the past two weeks.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt sad or down on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I think… probably, yes. I used to really look forward to things like softball, my book club, working in my garden, even helping with the robotics club. It doesn’t really feel enjoyable anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I’ve had some rough days, but I don’t know if it’s most days or if I’d call it sadness exactly. And then it kind of spills over at home too, like I’m letting my family down or not being a good mother.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you been having trouble concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It’s more like I feel off and kind of weighed down sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen in your life before these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It seemed to start after I took over the STEM assessment redesign at school, on top of my normal teaching load. That was about a year and seven months ago. Since then, it’s just kind of been going on.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings and thoughts mainly happen when you’re dealing with the school assessment redesign or thinking about it?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, the assessment redesign definitely seems connected, but I haven’t really tracked whether it only happens when I’m dealing with that or thinking about it. It just feels kind of mixed into everything now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this way even on days when work isn’t involved at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t paid close enough attention to separate it out like that. I know work is a big part of it, but I can’t say for sure what it’s like on days when work isn’t involved at all.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say nearly every day. I’ve definitely felt down a lot over the past couple weeks, but I haven’t really kept track of whether it’s most of the day each day.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt hopeless?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve felt pretty low at times, but “hopeless” feels harder to pin down. I don’t know if I’d say that’s been there clearly over the past two weeks.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt sad or down on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I think… probably, yes. I’ve felt sad or down on quite a few days these past two weeks. I’m not sure I’ve counted it exactly, but it feels like more days than not.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ve been really tired, more than usual. By the time I get through teaching and then a department meeting, my body just feels heavy. Even making dinner or answering a few parent emails feels like climbing a hill.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ve been having trouble sleeping. I’ll be really exhausted, but then I lie there replaying assessment deadlines or things I think I messed up in class. And a lot of mornings I wake up around 4 and can’t really get back to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you been feeling worthless or like you’ve let other people down?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. When a lesson doesn’t go well, or a student looks confused, I immediately take it as proof that I’m failing them. I keep replaying it afterward, thinking I’m a terrible teacher. And then it kind of spills over at home too, like I’m letting my family down or not being a good mother.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you been having trouble concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It’s been hard to focus and make even small decisions. I’ll read the same student response three or four times and still not know what score to give it. Even simple things, like deciding which lab supplies to order, can stall me for an hour.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most school days I either forget to eat or I just avoid it until late afternoon. I’m not trying to lose weight, but over the past year my clothes have gotten noticeably looser.\n\nDoctor: Before this current stretch, was there ever another time in your life when you had a similar period of losing interest in things and feeling down for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve had stressful times before where I was down for a while, but I don’t know if it was like this or if it lasted at least two weeks. Nothing specific is coming to mind right now.\n\nDoctor: If the school assessment redesign were no longer part of your life, do you think these symptoms would still be there?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. The redesign has definitely been a big source of stress, but I don’t know if taking it away would make everything lift. I guess I can’t really tell.\n\nDoctor: Before this period began, was there ever a time in your life when you felt this same kind of loss of interest and low mood for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I’ve had times where I felt low or stressed, but I can’t remember a clear period before this where it was the same kind of loss of interest and low mood for two weeks straight.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
